History
Dorimara was born in the, as the Cult of the Absolute threatened Baldur's Gate's safety. Still a newborn, she resided in the Stonyeyes neighborhood with her mother. Dorimara became the first grandchild of Oliver Tefoco, who, as the city became closed off in fear of the cultists, was worried about visiting because of the closed city gates and of burdening his daughter, who had just given birth. Instead, Oliver sent his daughter a letter with several jingling coins, asking Bubbles to buy Dorimara a cute hat from her Pop-pop.

## Dornal Silverhand

Dornal Silverhand was a human ranger who lived in the 8 century DR and most notably fathered the Seven Sisters with Mystra while she possessed the body of his wife, Elué Shundar, in a dramatic series of events that led to her death at his hands. Enraged, Dornal scoured the North for twenty years until meeting his death in battle, being granted relief by the Goddess of Magic and continuing his existence as a being known as the Watcher.

History
Dornal Silverhand was born in the 8 century DR and was a noble whose family ruled over some lands near Neverwinter on the Sword Coast North, including Silverhand Tower. Dornal was a ranger, adventurer, and at one time in his life, a member of the Harpers.

The goddess Mystra, having decided to breed individuals that would be suitable to act as her Chosen, set her sights upon Dornal, as he was seen by her as the ideal human for her purposes. Dornal met the half-elf Elué Shundar, a reclusive sorceress known then as the Lady of the Gate. The two met in a brief battle, as Dornal had incorrectly believed her to be an evil sorceress, but was then smitten by her. Elminster later theorized that Mystra may have manipulated events in order to ensure their meeting.

Aware of the ranger's love for Elué, Mystra possessed her and seduced Dornal, who found his advances quickly and passionately rewarded. Mystra used Elué's magic to both strengthen and dazzle Dornal. While at first Mystra's possession of Elué happened without her knowledge, the use of the sorceress's powers forced the goddess to reveal herself to her, and Elué agreed to share her body with the Lady of Mysteries. The couple traveled together for two years across the North, until both relocated to Silverhand Tower. There, Dornal's ailing mother accepted Elué with open arms, as she had seen her in dream visions as the "sorceress in the moonlight".

Dornal and Mystra/Elué were wed in the Year of Drifting Stars, 760 DR. While living in Silverhand Tower for the next seven years, Dornal and Mystra (through Elué's body) gave birth to six daughters. They first welcomed Anastra Syluné Silverhand in the Year of Laughter, 761 DR; followed by Endué Alustriel (762 DR); Ambara Dove (763 DR); Ethena Astorma (764 DR); Anamanué Laeral (765 DR); and Alassra Shentrantra (766 DR), as well as conceiving Erésseae Qilué (767 DR).

During this process, however, Elué's body became increasingly withered and, by the time of her final pregnancy, she had effectively become a creature akin to a lich, held together only by Mystra's power. Meanwhile, Dornal had become increasingly unhappy. Not only did he wish to father a son as well as his daughters, he was dismayed at the way his beloved wife was decaying. Fearing her death, Dornal desperately sought to save Elué through magical means. The most powerful priest Dornal found was Unsible, a cleric of Tyche based in Neverwinter. He informed Dornal that his wife was being possessed by an intelligent and powerful force, though the cleric did not identify Mystra as the entity responsible.

Sickened, Dornal decided to relieve Elué's suffering by slaying her. Using a moonlit walk through a glade as pretext, Dornal cut off his wife's head. He was then confronted by Mystra, who'd been finally forced to reveal herself. Broken by what he'd done, and appalled by Mystra's manipulation of both him and his wife, Dornal was filled with bitterness at the goddess, who tried to console him to no avail. Seeing only two alternatives, to either destroy the ranger's mind and transform him into a witless servant, or to spare him and set him free, Mystra decided to let Dornal go, and went on to devise a plan to save the final unborn daughter's life, which succeeded.

In grief, Dornal walked away from his wife's corpse, and abandoned both his lands and children. For the next two decades, the adventurer went on a rampage across the North, attacking brigands and monsters, and especially evil mages whom he nurtured a deep hatred for, seeking to be killed by them in his reckless attacks. Mystra protected Dornal, helping him survive the battles throughout those years. Eventually, in the Year of the Hearthstone 797 DR, Dornal was mortally wounded while fighting singlehandedly against raiding orcs on a hillside. Having deduced the identity of the goddess  that had protected him for so long, Dornal's last words were a plea for Mystra to remember him. Deeply touched by the gesture, Mystra offered Dornal a chance to exist as one her servants, which he accepted.

Assuming the identity of the Watcher, Dornal wandered Toril, seeking individuals with potential to be Chosen of Mystra, as well as identifying magical problems. The Watcher continued his service while largely unnoticed by mortals throughout the centuries, well into the 14 century DR. He was one of the few who understood the true value of the Seven Sisters.

## Doublecross bow

The crossbow had sensible wooden furniture with a top-loading bolt magazine. It was based on the same classic designs as drow hand crossbows, and used a nightshade or hemlock venom to incapacitate foes with a single hit.

Powers
The doublecross bow was magically enhanced to pierce through armor and cause crippling wounds in the target.

History
The crossbow, once of Faerûn's Underdark, emerged unexpectedly in the city of Stormreach in Eberron following the destruction of the Spinner's Prison in Khyber, when Lolth tore open a huge portal from the Demonweb Pits.

## Docent

A Docent was a kind of sentient magical object that could be used by warforged as an augmentation to their form. Though native to the isolated world of Eberron, similar artifacts were not unknown in the lands of Faerûn, either.

Description
Docents were typically embedded inside the body of a warforged, most often inside the chest. Eberronian Docents were usually made of metal, but objects that functioned similarly to them could also be made from gemstones, wood, stone, or dragonscales.

Powers
Docents could be made to impart almost any sort of magical effect, but most improved the bearer's resistance to physical and magical harm, acting not too differently in utility from a variety of other defensive magical items.

History
As warforged were not known to Faerûn, Docents were likewise an unknown concept. However, when some adventurers from Eberron arrived in the mid-1480s DR, various local magical artifacts were identified as similar enough to Docents as to be considered interchangeable.

Notable Docents
 An ancient gemstone, found in a Temple of Mystra.
 Entropic heartstone, a magical gemstone containing the power of Graz'zt.
 Heartwood, a pseudo-Docent made from wood.
 Livewood core, a piece of living wood from a druidic grove.
 The memory of fine regalia, a stone from Barovia imbued with haunting memories.
 Stone heart, a stone lump from the Demonweb Pits.
 Templar's docent, an Eberronian Docent used by knights of the Church of the Silver Flame.

## Dolindar Tomb

The Dolindar Tomb was a large crypt in the Shadowfell city of Evernight in which the Dolindar family was interred.

Location
The tomb was within Evernight's Graveyard. It was located away from the east wall, and was marked by a yard overgrown with weeds.

A secret "entrance" existed within the Pauper's Graveyard of Neverwinter on the Material Plane. Within a dusty and nondescript tomb—recognizable only by an inscription reading "Home Away, To Rest Forever"—was a Crevice of Dusk that connected to the final chamber of the Tomb.

Structure
The tomb was built of durable stone. Above ground, there was a single-story 20- by  structure with a portico on its east side. The entrance was a large stone door engraved with "DOLINDAR" that sat within this portico, and beyond the door was a steep spiral staircase that descended  into the tomb proper.

Interior
The chambers of the tomb were  high and lacked any light sources. Stone doors divided it into multiple rooms, and these doors scraped loudly when moved.

The main hall of the tomb contained ten niches in which nine lesser members of the family had been interred. Each niche was covered by a stone slab adorned with an image of the deceased, however any writing or art that might have identified the deceased had long since faded away by the late 15 century DR. The tenth, unoccupied niche served as the home of the tomb's ghost caretaker, Newmy.

Two staircases branched off from this main hall. The northern staircase led to a chamber that contained the stone coffins of the siblings Evisha and Nolan Dolindar, while the southern staircase led to a small, unlocked vault that contained the family's treasures sitting atop six stone pedestals. Each of these branching side chambers then rejoined via additional stairs to connect in the heavily trapped resting place of Kevetta Dolindar, the former matriarch of the family. Located within her stone coffin was the Crevice of Dusk to Neverwinter.

Atmosphere
The entire tomb exuded a sense of loneliness that could be felt even before crossing the threshold. This feeling was so strong that it isolated allies from each other, rendering them unable to motivate themselves to come to each other's aid.

History
The tomb was old and the Dolindars were long dead by the late 15 century DR. At this time, Evernight's more ancient residents (such as the vampire Sangora) still remembered the family and recalled tales of the Crevice of Dusk within their tomb.

Inhabitants
The tomb was cleaned and maintained by the ghost Newmy, however she did not dare enter the chambers of Evisha and Nolan—who had both arisen in death as lost sorrowsworn—or the tomb of Kevetta—who had become a lonely sorrowsworn.

## Dolindar

The Dolindars were a family of wizards who were banished to the Shadowfell sometime long before the 15 century DR. The family settled in Evernight, becoming a rarity in that city: living humans. There they studied arcane magic and came to be experts on the subject of interplanar travel.

Possessions

In addition to the Dolindars' modest wealth, they owned a number of family treasures, including: a copy of the book Out of the Endless Prison, a driftglobe adorned with a map of the gate-towns, a gold helmet, a necklace of adaptation inscribed with "My breath is yours, Kevetta—take it", and a snow globe containing a miniature replica of Neverwinter.

History
The reason for the Dolindars' exile was lost to history, but it was known that the family bore a curse that rendered them unable to use portals to travel between planes, thus trapping them in the Shadowfell.

At some point, the family built a tomb for themselves and managed to install a permanent portal to Neverwinter—known as a Crevice of Dusk—deep inside of it. However, they were evidently unable to use this portal to escape from Evernight owing to their curse. They subsequently hired a caretaker for the tomb named Newmy, and by paying her many lifetimes' wages up front, they bound her to maintain the tomb even after her death.

The family eventually passed away and were interred in their family tomb alongside their treasures. As of the late 15 century DR, Evernight's more ancient residents (such as the vampire Sangora) still remembered the Dolindars.

Members

The family was relatively small and insular. It included at least twelve members, all of whom were lonely and miserable following their banishment to the Shadowfell. These emotions would continue to haunt them long after their deaths.

Notable members included:
Kevetta Dolindar, the family matriarch. She arose in death as a lonely sorrowsworn.
Evisha Dolindar, an important daughter of the family. She arose in death as a lost sorrowsworn.
Nolan Dolindar, brother to Evisha. He likewise arose in death as a lost sorrowsworn.

## Dolan Cymrych

Dolan Cymrych was High King of the Moonshae Isles for a brief time in the mid–9 century DR.

Relationships
He was a member of the Cymrych Dynasty and the second son of his predecessor, High King Kevan. His successor was his first son, Conn Cymrych.

History
Dolan assumed the throne of the High King in the, following the death of his father.

Only two years later, in the, a massive fleet of Illuskan longships arrived from the Sword Coast seeking to invade and settle the Moonshaes. Dolan and his son, Prince Conn, led a flotilla of coracles to meet them in Whitefish Bay, but they were hopelessly outmatched and their fleet was all but destroyed. Dolan died in that sea battle as he covered his followers' and son's retreat, and the Crown of the High King sank with him. The loss of the Crown would severely weaken the political power of his successors, who would go on to fight and ultimately concede territory to the same "Northmen" invaders over the next century.

## Doomed Legion

The Doomed Legion, sometimes incorrectly referred to as the Legion of the Damned was a fighting force that served Price Ketheryll of the Moonshae Isles in the late 3 century DR.

History
The Doomed Legion was a highly successful military force that enabled Prince Ketheryll to go to war with his neighbors and conquer them. Notably, they expelled all of his human rivals from the Isle of Gwynneth. Not as widely known was their role in capturing King Durnhal and Queen Morgan of Corwell in the, whose downfall many attributed to betrayal rather than conquest.

On the night of the Summer Solstice in the, the inhabitants of the Castle of Skulls, including the Legion, wiped each other out in a gibbering, suicidal orgy of combat. Some suspected that the wizard Flamsterd may have actually contributed to this, causing them to disappear with the aid of the Earthmother  Some members of the Legion attempted to escape the Castle, but Ketheryll did not allow it and declared them to be deceivers and traitors.

Even over a millennium later, tales of the Legion's successes in their master's campaigns of conquests were widely known and accepted as true on Gwynneth.

Members
The Legion comprised Ketheryll's soldiers, lieutenants, and retainers. They all swore their lives to his service, but in truth were branded with his symbol (a skull) which magically bound them to his service. They were housed within the Castle of Skulls where they lived in cramped, low alcoves that dotted the perimeter of a large, arched room made entirely of bones.

It was said that a pastime enjoyed by the Legion was executing captives in the Circus Bizarre for the amusement of their master.

Notable members included Garius, a rogue who attempted to flee from the Castle of Skulls on the Legion's fateful last night and about whom there were tales suggesting that he had succeeded.

Doriak was a hunter of jungle beasts, and his favored quarries included big claw crabs, red recluse spiders, and venomtail scorpions.

He traded bait and lures to hunters who impressed him, such as by bringing him trophies of their hunts.

Doriak was a member of the Hunters' Guild of Chult, who congregated at Hunter's Square in Port Nyanzaru.

During the time of the death curse, Doriak was interested in helping other hunters to pursue a number of fearsome creatures, such as the manticore known as Rasalas, a massive big claw crab known as Karkinos, the giant spider called Chitters, and the fire scorpion Hedetet.

## Doomknight

Doomknights (or doom knights) were undead corruptions of previously righteous paladins.

Description
These undead were human-sized and visibly muscular. They seemed to be skinless in many places; however, how much skin was missing varied. Some body parts had their epidermis or dermis missing, while others had muscles exposed. These deformations looked like they were inflicted by burns or through skinning. Doomknights wore armor and a helmet made of an animal or monster's skull. It is possible that this helmet wasn't a helmet but a skeletal body party around their head. They had glowing orange eyes.

Personality
While doomknights were always chaotic evil, before a paladin was transformed into a doomknight, they had to be a previously righteous individual that fell from grace.

Abilities
Doomknights had high strength and resilience, an inherent minor resistance to spells and the ability to shrug off weak attacks and reduce damage from powerful blows. They possessed darkvision. A doom knight could use magic similar to dispel magic, fireball, power word, stun, and see invisibility, which they could do one to three times a day depending on the ability. They were able to create an aura of fear around themselves, which forced weak-minded opponents to flee from their proximity. Like many other undead, doomknights were immune to death magic, mind-affecting spells, paralysis, and poison. Because of an inherent strong alertness, doomknights were harder to ambush.

Combat

These undead almost exclusively used greatswords and heavy armor in combat. They were however proficient with all types of martial weapons. While fighting multiple opponents, doomknights were able to perform quick strikes against a new opponent after downing one. When fighting in groups, doomknights were often led by doomknight commander, which, in addition to the abilities of standard doomknights, could create a blade barrier. Doomknights used their available magic when fighting.

Behavior
Doomknights followed a simple militaristic structure, where they were led by commanders when in groups. When in groups or alone they were almost always aggressive on sight. Necromancers and blackguards that summoned doomknights via the create greater undead spell or create undead ability, respectively, could expect them to follow easy commands. Doomknights sometimes took control over lands.

History
Around the 1370s DR, one could occasionally encounter these undead all over the Sword Coast North in burial places or in the service of necromancers. Around the same time period, by using the spell Tenser's transformation, an individual could take on a form similar to an elven doomknight.

Ecology
Doomknights could either be found alone or in small to medium groups. They were almost always aggressive on sight. Some of the ways of creating a doomknight were using the create undead ability of blackguards or the create greater undead spell.

## Dorotee Leachlung

Dorotee Leachlung was a sea hag member of the Kraken Society and a member of the Sisters of Fury coven that aided the shaman Iceblood in his crusade against the Ten Towns in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Dorotee was horrific in appearance. She had a sickly yellow wart and sore-ridden skin. Dorotee's hair was long and lifeless and hung off her scalp like decaying seaweed.

Abilities
Dorotee Leachlung was a powerful hag who possessed all the powers and spellcasting abilities of a sea hag. She also was a priestess of Umberlee and was granted the Bitch Queen's divine magics.

Possessions
Dorotee carried a gem of seeing and kept a summoning focus in her lair that kept a powerful water elemental anchored to the Prime Material plane to serve as her bodyguard.

History
In the, the Sisters of Fury joined Iceblood, the mysterious priest of Arctus, a demigod child of Auril, on his crusade across Icewind Dale. The hags of Dorotee's covel established lairs across Icewind Dale and began weaving their spells and expanding influence over local monsters. Dorotee Leachlung established her lair in a sea cave in a tiny rocky islet just off the coast of Maer Dualdon just east of the town of Targos. From there, the hag spread chaos through her sahuagin servants. Secretly from her coven, Dorotee was a member of the Kraken Society and served kraken Slarkrethel and High Captain Rethnor of Luskan. For the time being, the Society's goals aligned with Iceblood, but Dorotee was to stop the shaman's activities south of the Spine of the World as the city of Luskan was still recovering after the war with Neverwinter.

Sometime before the Sisters of Fury arrived at Icewind Dale, the night hag named Agaza took residence in the Lonely Wood and did not like other hags encroaching on her territory. When the famed Hero of Neverwinter found Agaza's lair, she convinced them to hunt Dorotee and the Sisters down. Subsequently, the Hero and their comrades stormed Dorotee's lair and ended her evil via decapitation. The Sisters's heads were used by Agaza as sacrifices to perform a ritual that, as the Hero was led to believe, would restore their memories, but, in truth, used to summon Gorgomorz, the Daggerford's Devil, back to Prime.

## Douver

Douver was a half-elf bard and member of the Three Coins Fellowship, an adventuring company active in the Delimbiyr Vale in the late 14 century DR, alongside Kars and Eman.Although described as a bard, Douver is actually statted as a Sorcerer 1/Rogue 1 on p. 22 and briefly listed as a Bard 1/Rogue 1 on p. 6. The former is presumed to be the correct version, though he has no skills in performance.

History
Only newly formed in the summer of the, the Three Coins Fellowship visited the city of Loudwater in early Eleasis. They had heard the story of a ghost that wandered Tragedy's Grotto after each Shieldmeet and of how those who followed it were said to find great treasure, or else sorrow and doom. They, of course, wanted the treasure, and were driven largely by this greed. Strangely, they'd heard this story from a feminine voice in their heads, one calling itself Tymora, the goddess of luck, and promising enough riches to become kings. They had little plan beyond that.

In the streets of Loudwater late one night, the trio encountered the ghost for themselves, finding it to be the spirit of a robed monk. It ignored them and they followed it to Tragedy's Grotto and the hoped-for treasure. When another band of adventurers came across them, the trio had no desire to share and would refuse conversation and assistance with increasing vehemence. Douver politely asked them to mind their own business.

One way or another, the Three Coins Fellowship and the other adventurers followed the ghost to Tragedy's Grotto. There, it sunk into the ground and the buried vault there. Then both groups were ambushed by groundlings in Zhentarim employ, with Douver, Kars, and Eman being targeted as they were first to arrive.

If they survived the battle, Douver, Eman, and/or Kars joined the other adventurers in exploring a buried vault the ghost had entered, finding no treasure but the mysterious shard of the Fallen One. However, one of the three would become crazed, being possessed by a magical force of lawful and evil nature and attacked the others to seize the shard, whatever the cost. If subdued and questioned, he would be confused and have no memory of it. They might be persuaded to speak of the voice they'd heard, but if forcefully interrogated or magically compelled to explain why he'd attacked the others, he would be possessed again, screeching fiendishly the cryptic words "Because the key is mine!" He would then be choked to death from the unseen force; only a protection from evil or protection from law spell could prevent it. Should he be rescued, he would reveal a cruel and evil presence had been in his mind. The other adventurers took the shard and followed the trail of its mystery to the Phantoms' Cloister.The identity of this apparent possessing force is unknown—the module says this will be explained in the second part, Secret of Phantoms' Cloister, but this was never published. Being lawful evil and "fiendish", it may be a devil.

Possessions
Douver fought with a masterwork rapier and a light crossbow, and wore padded leather armor. He also had a wand of cone of frost.

Abilities
He was skilled in areas of thievery, diplomacy, and spellcraft, and knew the arcane spells burning hands and shield, in addition to a few cantrips.

## Dolthin

Dolthin was a Shadovar soldier turned mercenary archer, who ended up working for the mercenary leader Dom in Cormyr during the mid-1480s DR.

Description
Dolthin was pale, heavily scarred, and had a shaven head. He wore a chain shirt and carried a bow.

Relationships
Dolthin's ties and loyalties to Dom were inscrutable from the outside of their group.

History

Dolthin originally served as a soldier in the army of Risen Netheril, which came to an end when he was slain during a disastrous battle. Through mysterious means he was resurrected and came to serve the infamous Dom as a mercenary. Coming full circle, they eventually took a job from Netheril to seize a piece of the Abyssal Planus Mechanus Nether Scroll from the Harpers as Bensen Virandson moved it down Greenweald Lane. Working alongside agents from Netheril and a small army of other mercenaries, Dolthin took up a sniping position above the Lane, only to be slain again in the ensuing battle.

## Dom (mercenary)

Dom was a proficient and fearsome mercenary who found work in Cormyr during the mid-1480s DR.

Description
Dom appeared a dashing and handsome man with neat dark hair and moustache, wearing a waistcoat.

Personality
Dom was a charming and pleasant man, but could flip to violence and cruelty as needed. He was a master manipulator of people, using these talents on friends and enemies alike, and kept his own personal goals and values secret and hidden. Though very cunning and intelligent, his erratic behaviour led to the idea that he was mad in some way—perhaps explaining his fondness for drinking wine from the skulls of his enemies.

Abilities
Dom possess magical powers, possibly of divine origin. He could call forth flame strike, searing light, poison, and could teleport Slam about tactically in combat.

Relationships
Dom kept a close circle of elite mercenaries by his side, each with ties to him that ideally ensured their loyalty and effectiveness. He maintained a friendship with the earth elemental Slam by speaking Terran, manipulated Rand with promises of finding his nephew Stiev, led the childlike Song along with a lifelong friendship, sheltered the drow renegades Udere and Pelloth, freed Thoven from slavery, and had mysterious ties to "Pep-Pep" Dolthin. His sole miscalculation was trusting in his influence over the bard Nil, who posed as a fool easily led by strong personalities, only to be secretly spying on him and assisting the Harpers.

History

Following the Shadovar invasion of Wheloon, the forces of Returned Netheril pursued Oriphaun Huntsilver as he absconded from Wheloon Prison with the Abyssal Planus Mechanus Nether Scroll. As he approached the Storm Horns via Eveningstar, Oriphaun noticed the Shadovar pursuit and tore the Scroll into pieces, hiding them along the High Road in order to confuse the pursuit and buy time for the Harpers. The Netherese drew up a significant force of hired mercenaries to control the road, going largely uncontested as the bulk of Purple Dragons and War Wizards in the area were devoted to fighting against the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar. Dom and his group were assigned to assisting the Netherese in the King's Forest, where one such piece had been detected.

Joined by Netherese wizard supervisors and many more hired sellswords, Dom and his team aimed to intercept the Harper Bensen Virandson on Greenweald Lane, and so set up several blockades on the road and spread out into the surrounding woodlands. Bensen and his escorts managed to fight their way through regardless, slaying most of his forces, with Rand surrendering out of disloyalty and Nil revealed to be a double agent who had reported to the Harpers. With his allies gone, Dom and Slam beat the Harpers to their destination—the hidden Harper Lodge—and awaited in the basement for the Scroll to be delivered to him. Despite the best efforts of man and elemental, his last gambit  was unsuccessful and he perished fighting there.

## Docker's quaff

Docker's quaff was a type of cheap meal commonly available for purchase in the City of Splendors, Waterdeep, for a mere copper piece.

Description
This meal was available to the poorest of the city inhabitants and adventurers down on their luck. This was considered to be the main meal of the day for many Waterdhavians. It consisted of a waterskin of watered-down barley beer and a loaf of dark, crusty bread baked with sausage ends and other types of meat scraps from the local eateries. Docker's quaffs were sold at the City of Splendors's open markets daily, from the early glimpses of the sun to early afternoon.

## Doreta

Doreta was a young woman from Ravens Bluff in the mid-to-late 14 century DR, who was taken by the vampire Romanisk Vlaensk for companionship.

Description
When adventurers chanced upon her, she was pale, very pale, after having been underground for weeks.

Personality
She was not accustomed to fanciful living, but had taken to it easily, trying to convince herself she did not miss her past life.

Relationships
The vampire Romanisk Vlaensk used her for companionship when it pleased him; he had refrained so far from draining her life energy for that reason. If his charm over her was broken, she wanted to return to her mother.

History
After Romanisk was freed from under Stag Falls castle by digging done at the behest of Baron Vilhelm von Stag Falls, he quickly turned the noble into an undead under his control, and had many people from the village taken to the castle. Doreta in particular was taken for companionship, and her family did not object, as they could tell she'd be taken whether they objected or not.

She remained with the vampire underground afterwards, being charmed to ensure her compliance, though kept in the lap of luxury.

## Donalbain

Donalbain was the founder of the bardic college at Broadleaf Lodge during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Activities
He was known to host debates, discussions, and lectures in the college. In cases when evil arose to threaten the Ffolk, he also hosted meetings of concerned bards in which they nominated spies to battle such threats.

He was happy to open his doors and share his resources with other bards, but he expected any visitors to respect and to share in kind.

Relationships
He employed three women from nearby Brannoch to help maintain his college: Branwyn, Dara, and Shannon. He was fond of all three, and would do anything necessary to protect them if the Lodge came under attack.

History
Donalbain acquired the rundown building of Broadleaf Lodge in the, and proceeded to repair the structure and establish his bardic college. The largest renovation was to transform the top floor of the Lodge's tower into a music hall, which required him to hire master craftsmen from Callidyrr to construct and plaster the room to create ideal acoustics. As magnificent as this music hall was, Donalbain's pride and joy was instead the lore hall on the level below it: this library contained a magnificent collection of knowledge, maps, and magic. However, as the library attracted more and more bards seeking to use it for their research, Donalbain was forced to apply magic mouths to each and every book and scroll that would raise objections if ever a bard "accidentally" tried to leave with it. In the, he created and stocked a hidden bolt hole under the floor of the great hall as a place to hide in cases of emergencies.

By the, his bardic college was bustling. It served as a meeting place for Ffolk bards and their allies, as well as a stop for famous performers to give concerts to the public of the Moonshaes.

## Donald Mac Innis

Donald Mac Innis was king of Langmuir on the island of Moray in the Moonshae Isles during the late 15 century DR.

Relationships
He ruled with the aid of his council, which included wealthy merchants, minor nobles, and his own sons, notably Keeron Mac Innis.

History
As of, King Donald was old and sickly, and had come to defer leadership of Langmuir fully to his council.The events of Darkness Falls over Moray are stated to take place one year after the events of Black Blood, which is presumed to take place in 1479 DR.
